# The registry of experiments

This is the registry of the project's experiments: proposed, running, done, superseded and abandoned. It is for agents and for the user: what was asked, what was run, where the scripts and logs are, and what came out.

Written 2026-10-20 from the experiments' READMEs, [the evidence](../design/evidence.md), [the state](../state.md), the reviews, the plans, the open issues and the explorations. The measurements themselves stay where they are; this page points to them.

## How to use it

- **Each experiment has an ID, `E-n`.** IDs are stable and never reused. They were assigned in document order on 2026-10-20; a later experiment takes the next free number, whatever its area.
- **A new experiment gets a row before it is run,** with status proposed or running and the question it answers. Name the decision criterion if there is one. Commit the row before the run; where the build is in flight, the row says so and names the commit afterwards.
- **A session's row names its baseline:** the last build of the same line it was measured on (the morning's unified build for the head), beside the load's control.
- **A criterion crossed by more than its own margin ends the line of work** until the row's "would change" branch is taken or the user decides otherwise. A change across Base gets a static census before its build (the methods it touches, the ambiguities, the instances it shares). From [the performance review of 2026-11-09](../reviews/2026-11-09-performance-day.md) H3, M3 (mine).
- **When it is done,** fill in the outcome with the key number(s), the date or build, and where it is written up.
- **When a later experiment or a design change replaces it,** set the status to superseded and name what replaced it. Do not delete the row.
- **An outcome says what a document states.** Where no document states one, the row says "not documented".

**Statuses.**
- **The experiments to come serve [the goals](../goals.md):** a new row names the axis (G-n) it gives evidence for. Speed is measured as a control only (2026-11-10).
- **proposed:** written down, not started.
- **running:** started, no outcome yet.
- **done:** run, outcome recorded.
- **superseded:** replaced by a later experiment or by a design change.
- **abandoned:** dropped without an outcome.

**Builds and sessions.**
- **The round:** the measurement round of 2026-10-20, julia-1.13 3b8085c222, TraitWorld bed3782. Logs in `experiments/factscompiler/round/logs/`.
- **The build of 2026-10-19:** julia-1.13 61852d8fb1, TraitWorld 375a930.
- **The package prototype:** TraitWorld's own facts policy as a package, sessions 23–28, before the facts mode moved into Julia's compiler.
- **Sessions to dates:** s20–21 2026-10-15; s22–23 2026-10-16; s24–27 2026-10-17; s28 2026-10-17/18 (no session note); s29 2026-10-18 to 2026-10-20.
- **"Plain"** is the same code without traits; on the patched build it is patched-plain, not stock.

**Paths.** `experiments/` is the experiments repository at the repository root (from here: `../../experiments/`). The demonstrators are sibling repositories (`../../TraitDifferentialEquations/`, `../../TraitMakie/`, `../../TraitReport/`, `../../forks/`).

## 11. The large-scale port: NetworkDynamics (W-32)

Upstream NetworkDynamics, SciML, GraphMakie and Observables, then the port a function at a time ([the meta plan](../plans/2026-10-21-networkdynamics-port.md)). Scripts and logs in `ndport/`, its own repository.

| ID | Question | Status (date) | Where (scripts/logs) | Outcome | Written up in |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E-151 | W-32 P1: the NetworkDynamics workload's baselines (tier S, upstream code without traits; [the P1 plan](../plans/2026-10-21-ndport-workload.md) §2). (1) Does tier S's first session run in under 2 minutes on the patched build? (2) Are the results the same in every setting: `env_port` exactly equal to `env_upstream`, and `all` and stock 1.13.2 within tolerance? (3) What does a change of equations (a new component function) compile again upstream, by package? **Would change the plan:** tier S over 2 minutes (trimmed by §2.4's rule); P0 differing from U in an exact field, or any setting failing a tolerance field (a bug, fixed before P3); the stack failing to precompile or run under `all` (a work item ahead of P3); "changed" compiling under 2 s upstream (then P4 aims at loads and first calls, not at the rerun). | done (2026-10-21, julia-1.13 6d480d0753, TraitWorld e9f4bfe) | `ndport/` (its own repository, not pushed): `scripts/workload.jl`, `workload_steps.jl`, `run_workload.sh`, `compare_digests.py`; `logs/setup/precompile-upstream.log`, `precompile-port.log`; `logs/p1-*/` | (1) yes: a session takes 70–73 s in every setting. (2) All 25 digests equal bit for bit in U, P0, A0 and S. (3) New equations: 15.6–16.0 s of compiling upstream, 6,810 new CodeInstances: fixed point ~2,700 (NonlinearSolve, SciMLBase, NetworkDynamics), solves ~2,700 (OrdinaryDiffEqCore, SciMLBase, DiffEqBase), access ~1,070; Makie almost none. The stack precompiles under `all` (476 packages, 354 s); loads take 11.9 against 5.4–5.6 s. The plan stands: P4 aims at the rerun | [evidence §3](../design/evidence.md#3-first-calls-and-sessions); `ndport/README.md` |
| E-152 | W-32 P4, step 1: NetworkDynamics' component dynamics behind `ComponentDynamics`, the sequential and threaded batches erased, in the package wrapped as a trait module ([the traits plan](../plans/2026-10-21-ndport-traits.md) §2). Tier S, product setting, strict mode off, fixed load order, two runs per setting; P against U (results), N (E-153, the trait's own effect) and P0 (E-151). (0) Is the network's type the same for both equations? (1) Are the digests and the right-hand-side checks exactly equal to U? (2) How many new CodeInstances of SciML's packages and SymbolicIndexingInterface does the "changed" pass create in steps b–e (upstream about 2,300 in c–e)? (3) How long does it compile there (N: about 15 s)? (4) Is each steady-state step within 10 % + 5 ms of N? (5) What does the right-hand side cost and allocate per call? (6) Does ND's test subset pass as at `upstream`? Measures library trait code calling plain user functions (interop). **Would change the plan:** different network types, or an exact difference (port bugs); more than 50 such CodeInstances (the attribution names the leak; `ODERHS` may move up); a confirmed steady-state step outside the margin (the port pauses; open issues §4 item 35); allocations beyond one per pass plus one per crossing (a port bug). | done (2026-10-21, NetworkDynamics trait-port c9339fd, TraitWorld a373e30, Static.jl c34adbb) | `ndport/` (its own repository): `scripts/rhs_check.jl`, `nd_tests.jl`, `diag.jl`, `run_workload.sh` (setting P); `env_p4`, `env_ndtest_*`; `logs/p4-1/`; the worktree `pkgs/NetworkDynamics-p4`, branch `trait-port` | Changing the equations no longer changes the network's type (`sametype=true`): the changed pass's steps b–e compile 0.43–0.49 s against ~15 s (N 14,970 ms), with no new CodeInstance in SciML or SymbolicIndexingInterface (U ~2,300). Digests bit-equal to U and N (two runs); `rhs_check.jl` equal to U in every execution style. The steady state fails its bound: step d 46.8 against N's 15.3 ms (three runs); a right-hand side 32 allocations and 3.1 µs against 0 and 0.5 µs: each crossing into a family member's bounded function from trait code costs ~13 allocations and 0.8 µs (2 and 0.28 µs from plain code): the entry's generic path (lowering §9.3) has run-time sites when compiled by facts. The methods each loaded package adds, and how many of them extend functions it does not own (external methods, each activated by a search of what it invalidates), for stock SciML and CairoMakie (S), the facts-compiled core (C), the demonstrators (D) and E-171's ScaleL; against each load's time and profile (E-169). julia-1.13 252edca933 (S: stock), TraitWorld e6d5976; one session each. **Would change the plan:** a trait world's load low because its packages add few external methods (ScaleL: 0.30 s for 86 MB of native code, against stock's 3.3 s) makes the port's narrow interfaces a load measure, and the Makie port's first aim; no such relation leaves the load to Julia's loader (open issue 73's other options). | **done** (2026-10-27; counts from one session each; C's and ScaleL's load times in these sessions invalid: stale images) | `experiments/ttfx/probe_external.jl` | Methods added by the load (external): S 80,263 (27,681), C 81,929 (27,944), D 9,196 (3,806), Dp 6,925 (3,086), ScaleL 5,896 (2,610); load 3.52, 3.85, 0.45, 0.16, 0.30 s. The load follows the stack's size in methods, which is its dependency tree (Makie reaches Distributions, SparseArrays, DataStructures, Unitful); a trait world is cheap because it is small, not by a mechanism of its own (ScaleL's external methods are its registrations with TraitWorld). The facts mode's load protection is the gain on this path, and large where the plotting library is compiled by facts; its price is A's load (8.2 s against 3.6 s). With split caches S and C take 5.45–5.55 s; order B costs S 0.36–0.38 s recompiling, C nothing. | `experiments/ttfx/README.md` (E-177) |

| E-214 | W-79's D3 ([the memo](../plans/2026-10-28-dataframes-lean-core.md) §5): the kernels' steady-state costs, forks against upstream (plain mode, the same build), on 10⁶ rows: `sort` on an integer column and on a string and a float column; `groupby`-`combine` with `sum` on an integer key, on two keys, on a pooled key; `describe`; `innerjoin` on an integer key; `combine` with a function of each group's `SubDataFrame` (1,000 groups). The forks pass `Dynamic` columns to the kernels; where one is slower by more than 20%, the same with the column opened at the kernel's entry (`compiled_for`, the memo §3.1). Minimum of 5 timings after a warm-up, one process per setting. **Would change the design:** a steady-state slowdown above 20% against upstream with columns opened. | **done** (2026-10-28, julia-1.13 a4e9ad5da9, TraitWorld 3fcff02 then bdaf4b3, forks 4d2bd5c and after; one process per setting and step) | `forks/scripts/d3_kernels.jl`, `d3_split.jl`, `d3_refcodes.jl`, `d3_profile_groupby.jl`; `forks/logs/d3/` | As the forks were (`Dynamic` columns): `groupby`-`sum` 35 ms against upstream's 2.6, on a pooled key 48 against 1.8, `mean∘skipmissing` 14 against 1.7, `describe` 2.2×, a two-column `sort` 2.5×. Three causes, three fixes: (1) a closure in the forks' `_group_rows!` boxed a counter and with it the function's locals (a plain-Julia bug of the port; `groupby` 28 → 3.6 ms); (2) the columns not opened at the kernels' entries (now `compiled_for`, the memo §3.1: the sorts 742 → 314 ms); (3) the aggregates and `describe` copied every cell into a `Vector{T}` for plain code, since §7.4 refused a view of a trait object as an `AbstractVector` (open issue 96, decided and built: `CellVector`). After: `sort` 68 and 314 ms against 141 and 303; `innerjoin` 26 against 27; `mean∘skipmissing` 1.59 against 1.71, no allocation; `describe` 77 against 53 (1.46×); `groupby`-`sum` 4.4, 7.1 and 4.2 ms against 2.6, 3.3 and 1.8 (integer key, two keys, pooled key): the per-column codes written out before they are combined, the price of compiling per column type rather than per tuple of column types (the memo §4, risk 1). | [evidence](../design/evidence.md) §3; `forks/README.md` (D3) |

## 12. Unification (W-83)

The quick runs of [the unification plan](../plans/2026-10-29-unification-evidence.md) §2.2 (the user, 2026-10-29: "Run U6 and U5 and the other cheap measurements."). The U-names are [the exploration's](../explorations/abstract-types-as-traits.md) (§10, §11.5, §13.6). Scripts and logs in `experiments/w83/`. E-259 (U9) decides between the options of [open issue 136](../open-issues.md) (level 2 after U6). From E-260 on: the unified build ([the synthesis plan](../plans/2026-11-05-unification-synthesis.md); W-97 to W-104), scripts and logs in `experiments/unification/`.

| ID | Question | Status (date) | Where (scripts/logs) | Outcome | Written up in |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| E-252 | U6, Lemma M's premise (exploration §11.5): is an extension loaded before a dependent's code compiles when all its triggers are in the dependent's tree? Four scratch packages: `X` (a set and a method), `T` (a type), the extension `XTExt` (the membership), `P` depending on `X` and `T` with a precompile workload at `(T,)`. (1) In `P`'s precompilation, did `XTExt` load before the workload ran (`--trace-compile`, `Base.loaded_modules`)? (2) In a session loading `P` alone? (3) In a session that loads `T`, compiles a call at `(T,)` in `Main`, then loads `X`: one invalidation of `Main`'s code, as a plain method by an extension would cause? Also the variants: the extension triggered by `X` alone in `f`'s owner, and a third package's precompilation. Today's build; product setting. **Would change the plan:** any order in which the extension loads after the dependent compiled at `(T,)` breaks Lemma M: the coherence rule's cut is wrong, and the recommendation falls back to C2 (bounded methods only in the function's owner's package). | **done** (2026-10-29; julia-1.13 00dcc9994c; plain Julia, TraitWorld not used) | `experiments/w83/u6/` (`gen.py`, `run.jl`, `session.jl`, `all.sh`, `r12.sh`, `summary.sh`; `logs/<R>-<mode>.log`, `logs/R12.log`); experiments e784a90 | **Lemma M's premise fails for memberships declared in extensions.** It holds in every precompile mode and without images where the dependent is a package, neither the extension's parent nor one of its triggers, with every trigger in its tree (R1–R5, R9b, R10): the extension loads first. It fails in every mode in three allowed arrangements: the member's own package compiled before an extension it triggers (R6) or before its own extension (R7); an extension compiled before another extension whose trigger set is not a strict subset of its own (R8; R8b, with the superset, passes; without images R8 depends on the load order). And an image written before an extension existed is reused, the extension loading after the package (R12). In each failure the late plain method invalidated 3 MethodInstances (R8 loaded `Sp` first: the image's code rejected at load). The loader: a package's triggers are inserted after its own load (`base/loading.jl:2615-2631`) and never in its precompile child (`:3226-3229`); an extension's child loads only extensions with a strictly smaller trigger set (`:3306-3308`; `base/precompilation.jl:843-846`); an image lists the modules loaded while it was written (`src/staticdata.c:3400`). In R6–R8 the bounded method is `f`'s owner's in its package, so C2 repairs none. Product setting, strict mode off, one run per arrangement and mode, load about 2.5. | `experiments/w83/u6/README.md`; [the plan §6](../plans/2026-10-29-unification-evidence.md#6-results-of-the-quick-runs); [the exploration §16](../explorations/abstract-types-as-traits.md#16-what-the-quick-runs-found-2026-10-29); [open issue 136](../open-issues.md) |

(2) is the cost with the slot, (2)+(3) without it. An upper bound: a session pays only for the code it calls again. julia-1.13 00dcc9994c, TraitWorld 33dd55e, forks 77b9aab; two runs; the machine's load logged. After the move BaseTraits' methods invalidate none, `ldiv!`'s included; the load invalidates 1,081 instances against 1,070 without BaseTraits, the 11 being TOML printing code that loading Dates (the library's dependency) invalidates. The 1,070 are the compiler's own code, invalidated by the library's methods for its classifying interpreter. Matrix–vector `mul!` stays right after the load. | `experiments/unification/stack/README.md` |
| E-282 | W-114's BaseTraits against PE-21's trigger ("BaseTraits puts methods at sets on many of Base's hot functions"; the review of 2026-11-07, H4): **does plain code pay for BaseTraits' methods at sets and its memberships of Base's abstract types?** Base's test files `arrayops` and `dict`, included in a session that loaded TraitWorld with BaseTraits, against TraitWorld without it (e572379); the wall time of each include. One run each, the machine's load noted. **Would change the plan:** a difference above the noise (5 %): PE-21 moves to the work queue, and BaseTraits' memberships of Base's abstract types are reconsidered. | **done** (2026-11-07; `unified` 0ce5b7f752, TraitWorld 4be0097 against e572379; one run each, load 3.7–4.3) | `experiments/unification/stack/e282.jl`, `logs/e282*.log` | **No cost above the noise:** Base's `arrayops` 103.8 s with BaseTraits loaded against 103.5 s without, `dict` 24.1 against 24.5 s. Both files pass with it loaded, once compiler §5.2 asks a pair with a method at a set in the query's world (0ce5b7f752): before that, a `KeySet` and a `BitMatrix` read as ambiguous. PE-21 stays where it is. | `experiments/unification/stack/README.md` |
| E-283 | W-101's gate and cost ([compiler §3.3](../reference/compiler.md#33-the-code-slot); [the plan](../plans/2026-11-07-w101-the-code-slot.md) §4): **does the code slot keep the callers of a recompiled callee whose result is unchanged, and what does a call through it cost?** (1) The synthetic cascade (`slot_cascade.jl`): a plain callee recompiled when a method it dispatches to is added; a facts caller and a plain caller over it, in a session and in a package image. Counted: the callers' CodeInstances kept or compiled again, their results in the new world and in the old. (2) The cost: a call through a slot against a bound call, a `@noinline` callee in a loop, `JULIA_CODE_SLOT=1` against `0`; three runs each. (3) The session: W-106's two workloads and TraitWorld's tests, slot on and off: wall time, and the CodeInstances marked, kept and invalidated (the walk's log). `unified` with the slot, product setting, strict mode off. Measures plain code and facts code calling plain code. **Would change the plan:** (1) a caller compiled again with an equal result, or a stale result in any world: the slot is not done. (2) more than 1 ns per call, or more than 3 % on a workload: the slot is narrowed to calls from facts code to plain code (a deviation row), and PE gets the rest. (3) no CodeInstance kept in the workloads' loads: E-261's estimate is rechecked before more is built on the slot. | **done** (2026-11-07; `unified` 7500630036, product setting, strict mode off; (1) one run, (2) three runs per setting, (3) one run per setting, each setting with a depot of its own; load 2.5–4.5) | `experiments/unification/stack/slot_cascade.jl`, `slot_cost.jl`, `slot_session.sh`, `slot_reload.jl`; `logs/e283-*.log` | **The slot keeps what it should; a call through it costs 0.08 ns where calls are independent; the workloads give it nothing to keep.** (1) The gate passes: a plain caller chain and a facts caller chain over a plain callee are marked (4) and kept (4) through the callee's recompilation to an equal result, and run the new callee; the old world still sees the old; a different result compiles them again (4 marks made final); a caller loaded from a package image is kept. (2) A call through a slot: 1.418 against 1.336 ns where calls are independent (+0.08 ns, +6 %), 1.588 against 1.588 ns where each waits for the last. (3) Slot on against off: `columns` 4.4 against 6.0 s, `ode` 4.6 against 6.3 s, TraitWorld's tests 25.3 against 27.1 s: faster because a callee behind a slot is compiled at its first call, not with its callers, not because code is kept; with the slot off the system image still calls through its own slots (794 misses), so "off" is session code only. Kept: none in the workloads, 2 in TraitWorld's tests. Loading TraitWorld marks 208 of the 1,586 MethodInstances in its invalidation log; the 142 left marked are all the compiler's own code, which runs in its own world. Work, the load, the same work (`slot_reload.jl`): the load leaves the work's code valid. Base's `precompile` test, which builds package images, 251.6 s with the slot against 231.8 s without (+8.5 %), `core` 69.1 against 74.6 s (one run each, `logs/e283-basetests.log`). TraitWorld's image with the slot against without: 13.7 against 11.9 MB of machine code (+15 %), built in 25.9 against 24.2 s; `using TraitWorld` 2.11 against 3.52 s (one run each, `logs/e283-image-size.log`): a callee behind a slot is compiled at its first call, a bound call has the JIT compile its callees with the caller. **Branches:** (1) and (2) pass, the slot stays on every static call with only a result edge, its cost to image builds (+8.5 % in `precompile`) open, with the review of the machine-code cache (W-120); (3) no CodeInstance kept in the workloads, so E-261's estimate is rechecked in its own sessions, package loads after work, on the unified build (W-119, with the ports). | `experiments/unification/stack/README.md` |

DataFrames: 15,975 kept of 18,418; CSV: 4,797 of 7,144. The extension's image is loaded from its file; validation rejects one of its instances. **So the session's compiles in the forks' steps are what the workload never compiles:** the entry points it calls inside its `mktemp() do … end` closure are inlined there (no instance of `CSV.write(::String, ::DataFrame)` is made, though its callees are), the verbs it does not run (`select`, `subset`, `transform` with `ByRow`: DataFrames' internals at CSV's column types, 64 ms for `_add_col_check_copy` alone), the REPL's display path, and `describe`'s `get!` compiled per column element type (its closure captures `Type{T}`; fixed in the fork). Stage 4 is the workloads' coverage, at broad typical column types. | `experiments/unification/e289/README.md` |

| E-336 | **A call that reaches an instance wider than its own match keeps its signature on the backedge** (found by E-335's read: 1.5 s, of which 1.4 s regenerating machine code for code invalidated at load; the set-root report, `experiments/unification/audit/set_roots.jl`, puts 1,659 of the 4,194 instances invalidated at the goal's load under an instance at `IO`: `seekstart(::IO)` 584, `print(::IO, ::AbstractString)` 460, `setproperty!(::IO, …)` 304, `get(::IO, ::Symbol, ::Bool)` 88, `close(::IO)` 68, `ismarked(::IO)` 60). The rule that produces it: a method typed `io::IO` is compiled once, so plain code that knows its stream (`isvalid_file_crc(::IOStream)`, `seek(::IOBuffer, …)`) reaches the instance at `IO`; the forward edge keeps the call's signature, the backedge drops it (`ForwardToBackedgeIterator`), so a member's own method (TranscodingStreams' `seekstart`) invalidates every caller of the instance, also those whose signature it cannot meet. Vanilla Julia drops it too, for a `@nospecialize` method. `unified` after 1906196731: a call with one match, fully covering, whose instance's `specTypes` is not the call's signature stores its backedge as `(svec(sig), caller)`; a method inserted later invalidates that caller only where it replaces the method over the intersection of its signature with the call's (`_invalidate_dispatch_backedges`); a callee's own invalidation reaches it as an ordinary backedge. | E-352 | **The member hook in the compile queue** (compiler §10.4, C-31; lowering L-52's compiled use; the user, 2026-11-10: "Implement 1. 2. and 3. But no plan and review required."): `unified` on 352b916899, TraitWorld on 1ea6af9. Inference notes each (member type, set) a call it resolves rests on where no answer is kept; when inference returns to the runtime, before the code runs, the runtime asks the hook for each. A failure is kept, the CodeInstances that noted the pair are invalidated with their callers, and inference leaves a call at a failed pair to dispatch, which raises the `ImplementError`. The runtime keeps the state per (member type, set): in progress, passed, failed. Checks: a new gate `n24_hook_compile_queue` (a compiled first use of a failing pattern member raises `ImplementError` at that use only, in a branch not taken nothing; a passing member's compiled use is checked once; `struct S <: X` used compiled before its `@implement` raises, and runs after it), TraitWorld's suite, the battery, the forks' checks, TDE's suite; the goal's task, 3 runs, against E-350's 1.81–1.87 s. Would change the design: a regression of the goal's task beyond the noise (then: flagged, open issues §1); a check that cannot run outside inference's lock. Baseline: E-350. | done (2026-11-10) | `experiments/unification/gates/n24_hook_compile_queue.jl`, `e289/e352.sh` | **Built: a compiled use of a failing member raises the check's error at that use; a passing member is checked once before the code runs.** `unified` fcdfd0da36. Inference notes the member (`jl_member_note_use`); the runtime asks the hook when the outermost inference returns (`jl_member_queue_drain`) and keeps the answer per member and set. A use whose check failed stays a dynamic call: dispatch raises there, and a branch not taken raises nothing. The first build still raised `MethodError`: inference's method matching had cached the dispatch entry, so the dynamic call skipped the hook; the entry is now dropped for a failed member. Callers compiled while a check failed are compiled again once it passes (a member by its definition after its `@implement`). Gate `n24_hook_compile_queue` (its own hook, 5 cases) and TraitWorld's `test/core.jl`, `test/declared.jl` pass. Lowering L-52 retired; compiler C-31 narrowed to what is left: a call by `invoke` at the set's method asks no hook, and with threads another thread may run the code before the check ends. The goal's task (label `f-unified-w352`, idiomatic trait code, strict mode off, a warm-up and 3 runs alone, load 5.2–5.5): 1.827, 1.827, 1.839 s (imports 1.239, 1.231, 1.243) against E-350's 1.81–1.87 s. No regression; nothing flagged. Checked in one run `e289/e352.sh` (the binary built from `unified` fcdfd0da36's source, it reports 352b916899 with changes; TraitWorld 048c44e's source; TraitDifferentialEquations f903e40; load 1.8–7.8): the image and ambiguity gates 9 of 9, the forks' checks, TDE's suite, `strict_activation.jl`, `strict_load_membership.jl`, and the battery (gates 30 of 30 and 33 of 33, Base's `core`, `subtype`, `worlds`, `ambiguous`, `reflection`, `Compiler/inline`, `Compiler/inference`, `precompile`, the 20 acceptance cases, TraitWorld's suite with its strict run, the workloads traitworld, columns, ode) pass. Each in two forms: the declaration alone, and with the library's work on the member in its image (a table summarized, sorted and cut; a solve with Tsit5; with Tsit5 and Rodas5). Counted, in a fresh session after the library is loaded: the instances the package's load brings, by the module of their method and facts or plain; the bytes of its image files. The member's cost is the difference between the two forms. **Would change the design:** a member whose image carries the library's generic code again (instances of methods that do not have the member in their signature), or bytes per member far above the plain twin's, would be a cost of trait code's images to record against G-3. | done (2026-11-10) | `experiments/unification/w134/member/` (experiments 2999b6d) | A declaration alone brings 0 instances, in the trait form and the plain twin. The column member with its work at two cell types: 42 instances (17 by facts: TraitTables 9, its own 8; Base 21, Core 4) and +96.8 kB of image over the declaration, against the plain twin's 146 (Base 123) and +240.0 kB: 0.29× the instances, 0.40× the bytes. The parameter struct with a solve by Tsit5: 65 instances (26 by facts), +124.7 kB; Rodas5 beside it adds 3 instances and 14 kB. Instances that do not name the member: 14 (column, both forms) and 34 (problem), the workload's own calls into Base, but for one of TraitTables' methods at `Vector{Float64}`. No load ends an instance; each takes 3.2–6.2 ms. Neither result that would change the design appeared. `unified` 36b653bb3b, TraitWorld-unified 048c44e, TraitDifferentialEquations-unified 6658c65, TraitTables-unified 160639e, TraitReport-unified a4bcf33; the product setting, strict off, one thread; a build pass and 2 passes with the same counts; load 7.6–8.9 of 128 cores; idiomatic trait code against a plain twin (none for the problem) | [README](../../experiments/unification/w134/member/README.md); [evidence §1](../design/evidence.md#1-the-guarantee) |
| E-361 | **Trimmed binaries on the unified build, against plain Julia's** (W-135, E-96's binary and E-118's three; G-6). With `julia-unified`'s `test/trimming/juliac-buildscript.jl` under `--trim=safe`, one program each: (1) hello, as the control; (2) TraitMakie drawing lines and points to a PNG; (3) TraitDifferentialEquations solving Lorenz with Tsit5, and with Rodas5 through ForwardDiff; (4) TraitReport reading a CSV with TraitCSV, summarizing and plotting; (5) upstream CSV with DataFrames. Beside (2) to (4) the plain twin where one exists (PlainPlots, PlainTables). For each: whether it builds, what the trim verifier reports (errors and warnings counted, by the module of the call), what `verify_main` reports in a session and how its list compares with the verifier's, the binary's size, whether its output equals the session's, and what the author had to add (entry points, annotations, rewrites). **Would change the design:** a trait program that builds only with additions a plain twin does not need, or run-time sites of the trait mechanism itself (a cast, a member's check, the firewall's pointer) that the verifier refuses: each is a cost of the mechanism against G-6, recorded as a tension; a `verify_main` list that misses a site the verifier finds is a deviation of traits §16. | done (2026-11-10, and 2026-11-11 for (5), TraitReport's plot and PlainODE on the unified build; `unified` 564a2288e2, vanilla v1.13.2 45b6d15e34, TraitWorld 05991fc; one build each, load 2–3 of 128) | `experiments/unification/w135/` (README) | **No demonstrator builds under `--trim=safe` or runs trimmed to its end, in either form.** Verifier errors: table 8 (plain twin 10; vanilla 12), ODE 122 and 160 with the gradient (plain twin 14 on vanilla; PlainODE does not load on the unified build, where its function `compiled_for` is a reserved name), plot 34 (86; 88). Under `unsafe-warn` all build (2.1–5.0 MB) and each stops at its first run-time dispatch. `verify_main`'s entry points carry the table past the members' operations and the cast. The author added one entry point for a JLL's `__init__` (both Julias) and `add_entrypoints(verify_main(…))`. The second round (2026-11-11, load 3–6): TraitReport's task with its plot 44 errors (59 with entry points; plain twin 96, vanilla 98), stopping where the table stops; upstream CSV with DataFrames 770 on the unified build and 766 on vanilla, 7.7 and 6.8 MB under `unsafe-warn`, stopping in CSV; PlainODE, its `compiled_for` renamed, 14 on the unified build as on vanilla. Changed the design: GT-13; traits T-107, T-108; a trimmed image's modules and slots (compiler §2.6, C-53 retired). | [evidence §4](../design/evidence.md#4-builds-and-binaries) |
| E-362 | **Error messages for a fixed list of mistakes, in trait code and in plain Julia** (W-136; G-5). One small library (`Shape` with `area`, `perimeter` and a default `label`; generic code over it) written twice: as a trait module, and as an abstract type with documented generic functions. Against each, the same mistake, as a script in a fresh process: (1) a type that misses an operation; (2) a result of the wrong type, one that plain code tolerates and one that the library's code cannot use; (3) a wrong argument, at a library function and at an operation; (4) an ambiguity, for a type under two interfaces and between two arguments; (5) a pirate, with strict mode and without; (6) a method that breaks the contract after the type joined; (7) a type that never joined, and a member by its definition used before its `@implement`; (8) a member that joins while a loop compiled before it runs (E-358); (9) the library's author using what the contract does not give. The trait form on the unified build, the plain form on vanilla Julia. Recorded for each: whether an error or warning is raised at all, when (the declaration, the definition, the call), the exception, the message's lines and bytes, the stack's frames and where its first frame lies (the user's file or the library's), and whether the message names the contract (the trait, the operation, its declared signature) and the fix. **Would change the design:** a mistake where the trait form's message comes later than plain Julia's, names less, or does not come; a mistake the trait form adds that plain Julia does not have. Each is a tension against G-5 (a message the mechanism cannot give), or a deviation of traits §11 where the reference promises the message. | done (2026-11-11; `unified` dd3f6d839b, vanilla v1.13.2 45b6d15e34, TraitWorld 158d000; one run each, load 7 of 128; a first run on 564a2288e2 with TraitWorld 05991fc is kept) | `experiments/unification/w136/` | **Of thirteen mistakes the trait form reports five at the line that makes them** (a missing operation, two wrong results, a later method, the library's own use of a field), where plain Julia reports at a later call, raised inside the library or in Base under it (four), or not at all (one); two more by a warning at the membership, an error under strict mode. Its messages are 2 to 5 lines and name the trait, the type and the operation's declared signature; plain Julia's 1 to 11 lines, naming the function that failed. Two are the same with a hint. One is worse (an operation called outside its declared signature). Two failures exist only in the trait form (`struct S <: X` without `@implement`; a membership too new for running code). A pirate is silent in both unless strict mode is on. Changed the design: the form of a message (traits §11.3; six defects fixed in TraitWorld 6a8ee7f and 158d000 and `unified` dd3f6d839b: signatures as tuple types 12 to 0 over the trait runs); open issue 177; GT-14. | [evidence §4d](../design/evidence.md#4d-error-messages-against-plain-julias) |
| E-363 | **SciML's three error cases: upstream, the port, and the port's plain twin** (W-136, G-5; traits T-99; E-5's cases and E-90's comparison, on the unified build). The cases: (1) a right-hand side reads parameters the problem was never given; (2) a `Float64` cache in the right-hand side under a gradient by ForwardDiff; (3) the same cache under Rodas5's automatic Jacobian. Three forms: upstream OrdinaryDiffEq's solver packages on vanilla Julia; TraitDifferentialEquations on the unified build; PlainODE, the port's twin without traits, on vanilla. Measured as E-5 did: the message with its stack trace, in full and as the REPL limits it; its lines, bytes, frames and longest line; where it is raised and what it names. **Would change the design:** a port's message no shorter than its plain twin's shows that the difference to upstream is the small library's, not the traits'; a case the trait form reports no earlier than upstream is one the checks do not reach (E-90's README: inference does not prove that a conversion fails). Either narrows G-5's claim to mistakes against a contract. | done (2026-11-11; `unified` dd3f6d839b, vanilla v1.13.2 45b6d15e34, TraitWorld 99c4934, TraitDifferentialEquations 5e342f7; upstream OrdinaryDiffEqTsit5 2.1.4, OrdinaryDiffEqRosenbrock 2.7.4, SciMLBase 3.55.0; one run each, load 8 of 128) | `experiments/unification/w136/sciml/` | **The port's messages are shorter than upstream's and no better than its plain twin's.** In full, lines / bytes / frames / longest line: parameters never passed, upstream 72 / 10,725 / 28 / 3,903, the port 35 / 3,706 / 16 / 415, PlainODE 33 / 2,606 / 15 / 272; the cache under a gradient 106 / 14,380 / 45 / 5,688, 83 / 9,471 / 35 / 715, 87 / 8,079 / 37 / 758; under Rodas5 81 / 89,806 / 22 / 33,983, 81 / 7,410 / 34 / 689, 71 / 4,448 / 29 / 227. None of the three is a mistake against a contract: each is raised at run time in the user's function, and no check sees it. Upstream's hand-written explanations say more in two. Took the branch that narrows G-5's claim to mistakes against a contract. Changed the design: no hint at an operation that is another owner's function (traits §11.4; TraitWorld 99c4934: BaseTraits' hints on Base's `getindex` gave wrong advice); open issue 177 (d); T-99. | [evidence §4d](../design/evidence.md#4d-error-messages-against-plain-julias) |
| E-364 | **A small trait program as a trimmed binary: typed, and with values known only by their trait** (W-139, G-6; GT-13). E-362's library (`Shape` with `area`, `perimeter`, a default `label`; `report` over it), two members, and three `main`s: (a) every call at a concrete member; (b) a loop over a `Vector{Shape}`; (c) a member stored as `Dynamic{Shape}`. Each beside its plain twin (an abstract type), on the unified build and vanilla, under `--trim=safe`. Measured: whether it builds, the verifier's errors and what they name, the binary's size and the libraries it links, whether its output equals the session's. Then the same after each step of W-139. **Would change the design:** (a) not building shows a cost of the trait machinery in fully typed code, against the claim that fully typed trait code gives a small binary; what (b) and (c) are refused for is what the closed-build rule (a method at a trait compiled for the members the build has; a split over them for a value known only by its trait) has to remove. | done (2026-11-11; before: `unified` dd3f6d839b; after: 97861c6b31; vanilla v1.13.2 45b6d15e34, TraitWorld 99c4934; one build each, load 6 to 13 of 128) | `experiments/unification/w139/` | **With the closed build, a trait program with values known only by their trait builds under `--trim=safe` and runs, where plain Julia's abstract type is refused.** (a) typed: builds and runs before and after, 2,479,552 bytes (plain twin 1,942,688; vanilla 1,845,008). (b) the loop over `Vector{Shape}`: 8 errors before; after, 0 errors, 2,550,896 bytes, the session's output; the plain twin 2 errors on both builds. (c) the `Dynamic{Shape}` field: before, 0 errors and a `MethodError` when run (attributed calls the verifier passed over); after, builds and runs, 2,540,800 bytes. No LLVM linked. Found: the build's compile asked memberships in the compiler's own world, so no call was ever split in a build. The demonstrators are still refused (table 10, ODE 120, plot 46). A family `Scaled{T} where T`, nested, in the vector (`unified` b5c6ac2967, the build's rounds): builds and runs, 2,558,496 bytes. A `main` entered at a real prompt: built from the 8 definitions it depends on, 2,526,016 bytes (TraitWorld 633d6c0). `make_executable` and `make_library` (TraitWorld 19862aa): an executable of 2,543,792 bytes and a library of 2,392,456 bytes called from C; an untyped program refused with `BuildError`. Changed the design: compiler §7.6; traits §16. | its `README.md`; [evidence §4](../design/evidence.md#4-builds-and-binaries) |
| E-365 | **W-140's small cases: does a membership reach every caller once no call carries a guard** (W-140; G-1, G-8). Cases of seconds, each in a fresh process: (1) an abstract type against a trait with `Main` marked loaded; (2) code in a package image compiled where its callee had no method at a trait, then a membership, the method defined before the image's load or after; (3) a call cached before a membership at a method `f(c::C) where C <: X{T}`; (4) a function passed where a method at `Callable` does not match, then its method; (5) what the library's load invalidates in a fresh session; (6) two orders of E-358's composition, the trait packages, one pass; (7) a caller at `Any` and a wide instance when a new type joins. And the audit of the set-to-methods index against the signatures. **Would change the design:** a case that gives a stale answer with the walk invalidating; a load that invalidates code instances. The rows were written with the run, the same day; the rounds over the ports wait for the conceptually complete state | done (2026-11-11; before: `unified` 1b2f0a9e1e; the first build with the walk invalidating: a2ea2aff32; after: a19d937eaa, TraitWorld 4718b6e; one run each, load 7 to 18 of 128) | `experiments/unification/w140/`; `audit/set_index.jl`; gates `n25_image_before_set_method`, `n26_callable_late_method` | **Every case gives Julia's answer after; three gave a stale one before.** (1) settled wrongly before (a component of `All{AbstractVector{T}, PX} where T` tested with its variable free): the wrong method; fixed. (2) the method before the image's load: stale before, under "a membership invalidates nothing" too (the image's code had no guard and validation saw the same match); right after. (3) stale before: the method was indexed under no set. (4) stale on the build without guards until the edge to the function's dispatch. (5) 65 log entries, no code instance invalidated; 24 with the walk not invalidating. (6) on a2ea2aff32 each later member ended one instance, 19 in all (the walk's conservative path at `total(v::Vector{Shape})`): the criterion that would stop the work; with a trait as an invariant parameter not replayed, none, and E-358's counts (160 instances, 8.8 ms per member). (7) on a2ea2aff32 all ended; after, valid: a membership adds no method. The index: 247 (method, set) pairs in the ODE port, none missing. The gates 67 of 67, TraitWorld's suite 81 test sets, TraitTables' and the ODE port's suites, the documentation's examples and E-364's four programs pass | its `README.md`; [evidence §1](../design/evidence.md#1-the-guarantee) |

| E-367 | **A membership as a method: the trade-offs, stage by stage** (W-141; [the build plan](../plans/2026-11-11-membership-as-method-build.md)). Do the proposal's ten trade-offs stay manageable when it is built? **Would change the design:** a stage stopped by its check; the proposal is then not adopted, or adopted with the deviation named. Small cases only. | done through stage 2 (2026-11-12; `unified` b66cdc66f2 against b1c0b50f4c; one run each, loads five; load 1 to 8 of 128) | `experiments/unification/w141/` (probes, `costs.jl`, `edges_stats.jl`, `by_hand.jl`, logs); gate `n29_membership_method` | **No stage stopped; seven trade-offs to carry.** The spelling had to change: `Type{<:S}` makes insertion linear in a trait's members (12.6 ms each at 2,000), `Type{S}` is constant. Built: a method per membership, folds on Julia's own edges, a method written by a program declares the membership; gates 68, the suites, one composition order (160 instances, none lost). Costs: a declaration 30 to 40 µs against 18 to 36; loads equal (TraitTables 0.104 against 0.102 s, the ODE port 1.00 against 1.03 s); a membership with 2,000 negatives folded elsewhere 293 µs against 7.5. Sessions hold 0 to 6 folded negatives. The method's piracy rule agrees with the membership rule on 125 of 126. Not built: the method as the only record, taking a membership back. The code is not smaller yet (+332, -288 lines). **The review and the decision (2026-11-12; `unified` 8b4fde6e84):** a refused membership written by hand left its method in the table (`probe_refused.jl`); the member as an argument type inserts 2,000 abstract members at 0.52 to 0.57 ms each on plain functions (`probe_valuesig.jl`). Kept: the method as the implementation of the edges, about 70 lines fewer than cells; removed: the method written by a program. **A pirate's membership by the method's rule** (the user, 2026-11-12; TraitWorld a2ca7be on `unified` 7fee959383): refused under strict mode where it would change code compiled by facts, in a session and at an image's load; gates 69, TraitWorld 83 test sets, the stack's three strict gates pass. gates 69 pass, the nine `m3_*` fail as before; TraitWorld 82 test sets, TraitTables 3, TraitDifferentialEquations 34 pass (one run each, load 15 to 19 of 128). | `experiments/unification/w141/README.md`; [open issue 180](../open-issues.md#2-design); [evidence §1](../design/evidence.md#1-the-guarantee) |
| E-368 | **Base's and the standard libraries' surface under the fully typed report** (W-143; open issue 179). For each exported function, the signatures over twelve broad types (one argument, and pairs) at which it has a method and a result; `fully_typed` at each; the holes grouped by cause and by the function they sit in. **Would change the design:** holes spread over most of Base, with no few functions that carry them. A first run of minutes was made before this row. | done (2026-11-12; `unified` f6ad3008b4; one run each; load 2 to 5 of 128) | `experiments/unification/w143/base_surface.jl`, `logs/base.log`, `logs/stdlibs.log` | **The holes are concentrated.** Base: 837 exported functions, 6,179 signatures, 71.4 % fully typed, 20.1 % with a hole, 8.4 % where the report failed; 164 functions have a hole at some signature; the holes sit in 106 functions, of which five hold 73 % of the 11,819 sites (`typejoin`, `ntuple`, `setproperty!`, `rehash!`, `_growend_internal!`) and twenty 91 %. Five standard libraries: 183 functions, 474 signatures, 68.8 % fully typed. The kinds: paths that build an error, a field set by a name not constant, reflection, `IO`, logging. 166 of Base's 1,244 signatures with a hole have only a call at a union, which a build splits (T-107). The report is the session's: it also misses holes a build finds. | `experiments/unification/w143/README.md`; [open issue 179](../open-issues.md#2-design) |
